## Frequently Asked Questions

[](#frequently-asked-questions)

### What is the best PDF parser for RAG?

[](#what-is-the-best-pdf-parser-for-rag)

For RAG pipelines, you need a parser that preserves document structure, maintains correct reading order, and provides element coordinates for citations. OpenDataLoader is designed specifically for this — it outputs structured JSON with bounding boxes, handles multi-column layouts with XY-Cut++, and runs locally without GPU. In hybrid mode, it ranks #1 overall (0.907) in benchmarks.

### What is the best open-source PDF parser?

[](#what-is-the-best-open-source-pdf-parser)

OpenDataLoader PDF is the only open-source parser that combines: rule-based deterministic extraction (no GPU), bounding boxes for every element, XY-Cut++ reading order, built-in AI safety filters, native Tagged PDF support, and hybrid AI mode for complex documents. It ranks #1 in overall accuracy (0.907) while running locally on CPU.

### How do I extract tables from PDF for LLM?

[](#how-do-i-extract-tables-from-pdf-for-llm)

OpenDataLoader detects tables using border analysis and text clustering, preserving row/column structure. For complex tables, enable hybrid mode for +90% accuracy improvement (0.489 to 0.928 TEDS score):

# Batch all files in one call — each convert() spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader_pdf.convert(
    input_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    output_dir="output/",
    format="json",
    hybrid="docling-fast"           # For complex tables
)

### How does it compare to docling, marker, or pymupdf4llm?

[](#how-does-it-compare-to-docling-marker-or-pymupdf4llm)

OpenDataLoader \[hybrid\] ranks #1 overall (0.907) across reading order, table, and heading accuracy. Key differences: docling (0.882) is strong but lacks bounding boxes and AI safety filters. marker (0.861) requires GPU and is 1000x slower (53.932s/page). pymupdf4llm (0.732) is fast but has poor table (0.401) and heading (0.412) accuracy. OpenDataLoader is the only parser that combines deterministic local extraction, bounding boxes for every element, and built-in prompt injection protection. See [full benchmark](https://github.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-bench).

### Can I use this without sending data to the cloud?

[](#can-i-use-this-without-sending-data-to-the-cloud)

Yes. OpenDataLoader runs 100% locally. No API calls, no data transmission — your documents never leave your environment. The hybrid mode backend also runs locally on your machine. Ideal for legal, healthcare, and financial documents.

### Does it support OCR for scanned PDFs?

[](#does-it-support-ocr-for-scanned-pdfs)

Yes, via hybrid mode. Install with `pip install "opendataloader-pdf[hybrid]"`, start the backend with `--force-ocr`, then process as usual. Supports multiple languages including Korean, Japanese, Chinese, Arabic, and more via `--ocr-lang`.

### Does it work with Korean, Japanese, or Chinese documents?

[](#does-it-work-with-korean-japanese-or-chinese-documents)

Yes. For digital PDFs, text extraction works out of the box. For scanned PDFs, use hybrid mode with `--force-ocr --ocr-lang "ko,en"` (or `ja`, `ch_sim`, `ch_tra`). Coming soon: [Hancom Data Loader](https://sdk.hancom.com/en/services/1?utm_source=github&utm_medium=readme&utm_campaign=opendataloader-pdf) integration — enterprise-grade AI document analysis with built-in production-grade OCR and customer-customized models optimized for your specific document types and workflows.

### How fast is it?

[](#how-fast-is-it)

Local mode processes 60+ pages per second on CPU (0.02s/page). Hybrid mode processes 2+ pages per second (0.46s/page) with significantly higher accuracy for complex documents. No GPU required. Benchmarked on Apple M4. [Full benchmark details](https://github.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-bench). With multi-process batch processing, throughput exceeds 100 pages per second on 8+ core machines.

### Does it handle multi-column layouts?

[](#does-it-handle-multi-column-layouts)

Yes. OpenDataLoader uses XY-Cut++ reading order analysis to correctly sequence text across multi-column pages, sidebars, and mixed layouts. This works in both local and hybrid modes without any configuration.

### What is hybrid mode?

[](#what-is-hybrid-mode)

Hybrid mode combines fast local Java processing with an AI backend. Simple pages are processed locally (0.02s/page); complex pages (tables, scanned content, formulas, charts) are automatically routed to the AI backend for higher accuracy. The backend runs locally on your machine — no cloud required. See [Which Mode Should I Use?](#which-mode-should-i-use) and [Hybrid Mode Guide](https://opendataloader.org/docs/hybrid-mode).

### Does it work with LangChain?

[](#does-it-work-with-langchain)

Yes. Install `langchain-opendataloader-pdf` for an official LangChain document loader integration. See [LangChain docs](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/integrations/document_loaders/opendataloader_pdf).

### How do I chunk PDFs for RAG?

[](#how-do-i-chunk-pdfs-for-rag)

OpenDataLoader outputs structured Markdown with headings, tables, and lists preserved — ideal input for semantic chunking. Each element in JSON output includes `type`, `heading level`, and `page number`, so you can split by section or page boundary. For most RAG pipelines: parse with `format="markdown"` for text chunks, or `format="json"` when you need element-level control. Pair with LangChain's `RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter` or your own heading-based splitter for best results.

### How do I cite PDF sources in RAG answers?

[](#how-do-i-cite-pdf-sources-in-rag-answers)

Every element in JSON output includes a `bounding box` (`[left, bottom, right, top]` in PDF points) and `page number`. When your RAG pipeline returns an answer, map the source chunk back to its bounding box to highlight the exact location in the original PDF. This enables "click to source" UX — users see which paragraph, table, or figure the answer came from. No other open-source parser provides bounding boxes for every element by default.

### How do I convert PDF to Markdown for LLM?

[](#how-do-i-convert-pdf-to-markdown-for-llm)

import opendataloader_pdf

# Batch all files in one call — each convert() spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader_pdf.convert(
    input_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    output_dir="output/",
    format="markdown"
)

OpenDataLoader preserves heading hierarchy, table structure, and reading order in the Markdown output. For complex documents with borderless tables or scanned pages, use hybrid mode (`hybrid="docling-fast"`) for higher accuracy. The output is clean enough to feed directly into LLM context windows or RAG chunking pipelines.

### Is there an automated PDF accessibility remediation tool?

[](#is-there-an-automated-pdf-accessibility-remediation-tool)

Yes. OpenDataLoader is the first open-source tool that automates PDF accessibility end-to-end. Built in collaboration with [PDF Association](https://pdfa.org) and [Dual Lab](https://duallab.com) (veraPDF developers), auto-tagging follows the Well-Tagged PDF specification and is validated programmatically using veraPDF. The layout analysis engine detects document structure (headings, tables, lists, reading order) and generates accessibility tags automatically. Auto-tagging converts untagged PDFs into Tagged PDFs under Apache 2.0 — no proprietary SDK dependency. Use `format="tagged-pdf"` (Python/Node.js) or `--format tagged-pdf` (CLI). For organizations needing full PDF/UA compliance, enterprise add-ons provide PDF/UA export and a visual tag editor. This replaces manual remediation workflows that typically cost $50–200+ per document.

### Is this really the first open-source PDF auto-tagging tool?

[](#is-this-really-the-first-open-source-pdf-auto-tagging-tool)

Yes. Existing tools either depend on proprietary SDKs for writing structure tags, only output non-PDF formats (e.g., Docling outputs Markdown/JSON but cannot produce Tagged PDFs), or require manual intervention. OpenDataLoader is the first to do layout analysis → tag generation → Tagged PDF output entirely under an open-source license (Apache 2.0), with no proprietary dependency. Auto-tagging follows the PDF Association's Well-Tagged PDF specification and is validated using veraPDF, the industry-reference open-source PDF/A and PDF/UA validator.

### How do I convert existing PDFs to PDF/UA?

[](#how-do-i-convert-existing-pdfs-to-pdfua)

OpenDataLoader provides an end-to-end pipeline: audit existing PDFs for tags (`use_struct_tree=True`), auto-tag untagged PDFs into Tagged PDFs (`format="tagged-pdf"`, free under Apache 2.0), and export as PDF/UA-1 or PDF/UA-2 (enterprise add-on). Auto-tagging follows the PDF Association's Well-Tagged PDF specification and is validated using veraPDF. Auto-tagging generates the Tagged PDF; PDF/UA export is the final step. [Contact us](https://opendataloader.org/contact) for enterprise integration.

### How do I make my PDFs accessible for EAA compliance?

[](#how-do-i-make-my-pdfs-accessible-for-eaa-compliance)

The European Accessibility Act requires accessible digital products by June 28, 2025. OpenDataLoader supports the full remediation workflow: audit → auto-tag → Tagged PDF → PDF/UA export. Auto-tagging follows the PDF Association's Well-Tagged PDF specification and is validated using veraPDF, ensuring standards-compliant output. Auto-tagging to Tagged PDF is open-source under Apache 2.0. PDF/UA export and accessibility studio are enterprise add-ons. See our [Accessibility Guide](https://opendataloader.org/docs/accessibility-compliance).

### Is OpenDataLoader PDF free?

[](#is-opendataloader-pdf-free)

The core library is **open-source under Apache 2.0** — free for commercial use. This includes all extraction features (text, tables, images, OCR, formulas, charts via hybrid mode), AI safety filters, Tagged PDF support, and auto-tagging to Tagged PDF. We are committed to keeping the core accessibility pipeline (layout analysis → auto-tagging → Tagged PDF) free and open-source. Enterprise add-ons (PDF/UA export, accessibility studio) are available for organizations needing end-to-end regulatory compliance.

### Why did the license change from MPL 2.0 to Apache 2.0?

[](#why-did-the-license-change-from-mpl-20-to-apache-20)

MPL 2.0 requires file-level copyleft, which often triggers legal review before enterprise adoption. Apache 2.0 is fully permissive — no copyleft obligations, easier to integrate into commercial projects. If you are using a pre-2.0 version, it remains under MPL 2.0 and you can continue using it. Upgrading to 2.0+ means your project follows Apache 2.0 terms, which are strictly more permissive — no additional obligations, no action needed on your side.

# OpenDataLoader PDF

[](#opendataloader-pdf)

**PDF Parser for AI-ready data. Automate PDF accessibility. Open-source.**

[![License](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/5b60841bea9e11d9d0b0950d690c9bc554e06385634056a7d5d62a15d1a4eabe/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f62616467652f4c6963656e73652d4170616368655f322e302d626c75652e737667)](https://github.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-pdf/blob/main/LICENSE) [![PyPI version](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/62f4cc0dbe482bfe8c3824c13b1bc4b4eeaeabd9ab0ae69121e9ada0ae6ee622/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f707970692f762f6f70656e646174616c6f616465722d7064662e737667)](https://pypi.org/project/opendataloader-pdf/) [![npm version](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/e154455d3952d3edc2b6fe2ecde59f3da1d1c46c715fa5f69bc31250259bf07c/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f6e706d2f762f406f70656e646174616c6f616465722f7064662e737667)](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@opendataloader/pdf) [![Maven Central](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/4d6b56b5f764066e1e1fbd0b80aeb787a94a46300485902029aa52e93d084f2a/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f6d6176656e2d63656e7472616c2f762f6f72672e6f70656e646174616c6f616465722f6f70656e646174616c6f616465722d7064662d636f72652e737667)](https://search.maven.org/artifact/org.opendataloader/opendataloader-pdf-core) [![Java](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/dd5297ff15fec08be96f3952e4ba6e6325f842adc610bb999abdff08765a960a/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f62616467652f4a6176612d31312532422d626c75652e737667)](https://github.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-pdf#java)

[![opendataloader-project%2Fopendataloader-pdf | Trendshift](https://camo.githubusercontent.com/96c28b3c6187456c559dbaf68560b2d9256759277038e261ae91e94389414773/68747470733a2f2f7472656e6473686966742e696f2f6170692f62616467652f7265706f7369746f726965732f3231393137)](https://trendshift.io/repositories/21917)

🔍 **PDF parser for AI data extraction** — Extract Markdown, JSON (with bounding boxes), and HTML from any PDF. #1 in benchmarks (0.907 overall). Deterministic local mode + AI hybrid mode for complex pages.

*   **How accurate is it?** — #1 in benchmarks: 0.907 overall, 0.928 table accuracy across 200 real-world PDFs including multi-column and scientific papers. Deterministic local mode + AI hybrid mode for complex pages ([benchmarks](#extraction-benchmarks))
*   **Scanned PDFs and OCR?** — Yes. Built-in OCR (80+ languages) in hybrid mode. Works with poor-quality scans at 300 DPI+ ([hybrid mode](#hybrid-mode-1-accuracy-for-complex-pdfs))
*   **Tables, formulas, images, charts?** — Yes. Complex/borderless tables, LaTeX formulas, and AI-generated picture/chart descriptions all via hybrid mode ([hybrid mode](#hybrid-mode-1-accuracy-for-complex-pdfs))
*   **How do I use this for RAG?** — `pip install opendataloader-pdf`, convert in 3 lines. Outputs structured Markdown for chunking, JSON with bounding boxes for source citations, and HTML. LangChain integration available. Python, Node.js, Java SDKs ([quick start](#get-started-in-30-seconds) | [LangChain](#langchain-integration))

♿ **PDF accessibility automation** — Auto-tag untagged PDFs into screen-reader-ready Tagged PDFs at scale. First open-source tool to generate Tagged PDFs end-to-end.

*   **What's the problem?** — Accessibility regulations are now enforced worldwide. Manual PDF remediation costs $50–200 per document and doesn't scale ([regulations](#pdf-accessibility--pdfua-conversion))
*   **What's free?** — Layout analysis + auto-tagging (Apache 2.0). Untagged PDF in → Tagged PDF out. No proprietary SDK dependency ([auto-tagging](#auto-tagging))
*   **What about PDF/UA compliance?** — Converting Tagged PDF to PDF/UA-1 or PDF/UA-2 is an enterprise add-on. Auto-tagging generates the Tagged PDF; PDF/UA export is the final step ([pipeline](#accessibility-pipeline))
*   **Why trust this?** — Built in collaboration with [Dual Lab](https://duallab.com) ([veraPDF](https://verapdf.org) developers) based on [PDF Association](https://pdfa.org) specifications, best practice guides and expertise of the [PDF Community](https://pdfa.org/community/). Auto-tagging follows the [Well-Tagged PDF specification](https://pdfa.org/wtpdf/), validated with veraPDF ([collaboration](https://opendataloader.org/docs/tagged-pdf-collaboration))

## Get Started in 30 Seconds

[](#get-started-in-30-seconds)

**Requires**: Java 11+ and Python 3.10+ ([Node.js](https://opendataloader.org/docs/quick-start-nodejs) | [Java](https://opendataloader.org/docs/quick-start-java) also available)

> Before you start: run `java -version`. If not found, install JDK 11+ from [Adoptium](https://adoptium.net/).

pip install -U opendataloader-pdf

import opendataloader_pdf

# Batch all files in one call — each convert() spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader_pdf.convert(
    input_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    output_dir="output/",
    format="markdown,json"
)

[![OpenDataLoader PDF layout analysis — headings, tables, images detected with bounding boxes](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-pdf/main/samples/image/example_annotated_pdf.png)](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-pdf/main/samples/image/example_annotated_pdf.png)

*Annotated PDF output — each element (heading, paragraph, table, image) detected with bounding boxes and semantic type.*

## What Problems Does This Solve?

[](#what-problems-does-this-solve)

| Problem                                                                                                                                      | Solution                                                                                                                                    | Status                                       |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
| **PDF structure lost during parsing** — wrong reading order, broken tables, no element coordinates                                           | Deterministic local PDF to Markdown/JSON with bounding boxes, XY-Cut++ reading order                                                        | Shipped                                      |
| **Complex tables, scanned PDFs, formulas, charts** need AI-level understanding                                                               | Hybrid mode routes complex pages to AI backend (#1 in benchmarks)                                                                           | Shipped                                      |
| **Manual PDF remediation cost** — Accessibility regulations (EAA, ADA, Section 508) demand Tagged PDFs. Manual remediation costs $50–200/doc | Auto-tag untagged PDFs into Tagged PDFs (free, Apache 2.0). Foundation for PDF/UA workflows; full PDF/UA-1/2 export is an enterprise add-on | Auto-tag: Shipped. PDF/UA export: Enterprise |

## Which Mode Should I Use?

[](#which-mode-should-i-use)

| Your Document                       | Mode                      | Install                                    | Server Command                                                         | Client Command                                                                            |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Standard digital PDF                | Fast (default)            | `pip install opendataloader-pdf`           | None needed                                                            | `opendataloader-pdf file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/`                                          |
| Complex or nested tables            | **Hybrid**                | `pip install "opendataloader-pdf[hybrid]"` | `opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --port 5002`                                | `opendataloader-pdf --hybrid docling-fast file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/`                    |
| Scanned / image-based PDF           | Hybrid + OCR              | `pip install "opendataloader-pdf[hybrid]"` | `opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --port 5002 --force-ocr`                    | `opendataloader-pdf --hybrid docling-fast file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/`                    |
| Non-English scanned PDF             | Hybrid + OCR              | `pip install "opendataloader-pdf[hybrid]"` | `opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --port 5002 --force-ocr --ocr-lang "ko,en"` | `opendataloader-pdf --hybrid docling-fast file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/`                    |
| Mathematical formulas               | Hybrid + formula          | `pip install "opendataloader-pdf[hybrid]"` | `opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --enrich-formula`                           | `opendataloader-pdf --hybrid docling-fast --hybrid-mode full file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/` |
| Charts needing description          | Hybrid + picture          | `pip install "opendataloader-pdf[hybrid]"` | `opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --enrich-picture-description`               | `opendataloader-pdf --hybrid docling-fast --hybrid-mode full file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/` |
| Untagged PDFs needing accessibility | Auto-tagging → Tagged PDF | `pip install opendataloader-pdf`           | None needed                                                            | `opendataloader-pdf --format tagged-pdf file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/`                      |

## Hybrid Mode: #1 Accuracy for Complex PDFs

[](#hybrid-mode-1-accuracy-for-complex-pdfs)

Hybrid mode combines fast local Java processing with AI backends. Simple pages stay local (0.02s); complex pages route to AI for +90% table accuracy.

pip install -U "opendataloader-pdf[hybrid]"

**Terminal 1** — Start the backend server:

opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --port 5002

**Terminal 2** — Process PDFs:

# Batch all files in one call — each invocation spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader-pdf --hybrid docling-fast file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/

**Python:**

# Batch all files in one call — each convert() spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader_pdf.convert(
    input_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    output_dir="output/",
    hybrid="docling-fast"
)

### OCR for Scanned PDFs

[](#ocr-for-scanned-pdfs)

Start the backend with `--force-ocr` for image-based PDFs with no selectable text:

opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --port 5002 --force-ocr

For non-English documents, specify the language:

opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --port 5002 --force-ocr --ocr-lang "ko,en"

Supported languages: `en`, `ko`, `ja`, `ch_sim`, `ch_tra`, `de`, `fr`, `ar`, and more.

### Formula Extraction (LaTeX)

[](#formula-extraction-latex)

Extract mathematical formulas as LaTeX from scientific PDFs:

# Server: enable formula enrichment
opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --enrich-formula

# Batch all files in one call — each invocation spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader-pdf --hybrid docling-fast --hybrid-mode full file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/

Output in JSON:

{
  "type": "formula",
  "page number": 1,
  "bounding box": [226.2, 144.7, 377.1, 168.7],
  "content": "\\frac{f(x+h) - f(x)}{h}"
}

> **Note**: Formula and picture description enrichments require `--hybrid-mode full` on the client side.

### Chart & Image Description

[](#chart--image-description)

Generate AI descriptions for charts and images — useful for RAG search and accessibility alt text:

# Server
opendataloader-pdf-hybrid --enrich-picture-description

# Batch all files in one call — each invocation spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader-pdf --hybrid docling-fast --hybrid-mode full file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/

Output in JSON:

{
  "type": "picture",
  "page number": 1,
  "bounding box": [72.0, 400.0, 540.0, 650.0],
  "description": "A bar chart showing waste generation by region from 2016 to 2030..."
}

> Uses SmolVLM (256M), a lightweight vision model. Custom prompts supported via `--picture-description-prompt`.

### JSON Output Example

[](#json-output-example)

{
  "type": "heading",
  "id": 42,
  "level": "Title",
  "page number": 1,
  "bounding box": [72.0, 700.0, 540.0, 730.0],
  "heading level": 1,
  "font": "Helvetica-Bold",
  "font size": 24.0,
  "text color": "[0.0]",
  "content": "Introduction"
}

| Field           | Description                                                            |
| --------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `type`          | Element type: heading, paragraph, table, list, image, caption, formula |
| `id`            | Unique identifier for cross-referencing                                |
| `page number`   | 1-indexed page reference                                               |
| `bounding box`  | `[left, bottom, right, top]` in PDF points (72pt = 1 inch)             |
| `heading level` | Heading depth (1+)                                                     |
| `content`       | Extracted text                                                         |

[Full JSON Schema](https://opendataloader.org/docs/reference/json-schema)

## Advanced Features

[](#advanced-features)

### Tagged PDF Support

[](#tagged-pdf-support)

When a PDF has structure tags, OpenDataLoader extracts the **exact layout** the author intended — no guessing, no heuristics. Headings, lists, tables, and reading order are preserved from the source.

> **Output quality depends on tag quality.** Not all tagged PDFs are well-tagged. For PDFs with sparse or incorrect tags, the default heuristic mode or `--hybrid docling-fast` often produces better results.

# Batch all files in one call — each convert() spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader_pdf.convert(
    input_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    output_dir="output/",
    use_struct_tree=True           # Use native PDF structure tags
)

Most PDF parsers ignore structure tags entirely. [Learn more](https://opendataloader.org/docs/tagged-pdf)

### AI Safety: Prompt Injection Protection

[](#ai-safety-prompt-injection-protection)

PDFs can contain hidden prompt injection attacks. OpenDataLoader automatically filters:

*   Hidden text (transparent, zero-size fonts)
*   Off-page content
*   Suspicious invisible layers

To sanitize sensitive data (emails, URLs, phone numbers → placeholders), enable it explicitly:

# Batch all files in one call — each invocation spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader-pdf file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/ --sanitize

[AI Safety Guide](https://opendataloader.org/docs/ai-safety)

### LangChain Integration

[](#langchain-integration)

pip install -U langchain-opendataloader-pdf

from langchain_opendataloader_pdf import OpenDataLoaderPDFLoader

loader = OpenDataLoaderPDFLoader(
    file_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    format="text"
)
documents = loader.load()

[LangChain Docs](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/integrations/document_loaders/opendataloader_pdf) | [GitHub](https://github.com/opendataloader-project/langchain-opendataloader-pdf) | [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/langchain-opendataloader-pdf/)

### Advanced Options

[](#advanced-options)

# Batch all files in one call — each convert() spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader_pdf.convert(
    input_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    output_dir="output/",
    format="json,markdown,pdf",
    image_output="embedded",        # "off", "embedded" (Base64), or "external" (default)
    image_format="jpeg",            # "png" or "jpeg"
    use_struct_tree=True,           # Use native PDF structure
)

[Full CLI Options Reference](https://opendataloader.org/docs/reference/cli-options)

## PDF Accessibility & PDF/UA Conversion

[](#pdf-accessibility--pdfua-conversion)

**Problem**: Millions of existing PDFs lack structure tags, failing accessibility regulations (EAA, ADA/Section 508, Korea Digital Inclusion Act). Manual remediation costs $50–200 per document and doesn't scale.

**OpenDataLoader's approach**: Built in collaboration with [PDF Association](https://pdfa.org) and [Dual Lab](https://duallab.com) (developers of [veraPDF](https://verapdf.org), the industry-reference open-source PDF/A and PDF/UA validator). Auto-tagging follows the [Well-Tagged PDF specification](https://pdfa.org/resource/well-tagged-pdf/) and is validated programmatically using veraPDF — automated conformance checks against PDF accessibility standards, not manual review. No existing open-source tool generates Tagged PDFs end-to-end — most rely on proprietary SDKs for the tag-writing step. OpenDataLoader does it all under Apache 2.0. ([collaboration details](https://opendataloader.org/docs/tagged-pdf-collaboration))

| Regulation                           | Deadline      | Requirement                                     |
| ------------------------------------ | ------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| **European Accessibility Act (EAA)** | June 28, 2025 | Accessible digital products across the EU       |
| **ADA & Section 508**                | In effect     | U.S. federal agencies and public accommodations |
| **Digital Inclusion Act**            | In effect     | South Korea digital service accessibility       |

### Standards & Validation

[](#standards--validation)

| Aspect            | Detail                                                                                                   |
| ----------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Specification** | [Well-Tagged PDF](https://pdfa.org/resource/well-tagged-pdf/) by PDF Association                         |
| **Validation**    | [veraPDF](https://verapdf.org) — industry-reference open-source PDF/A & PDF/UA validator                 |
| **Collaboration** | PDF Association + [Dual Lab](https://duallab.com) (veraPDF developers) co-develop tagging and validation |
| **License**       | Auto-tagging → Tagged PDF: Apache 2.0 (free). PDF/UA export: Enterprise                                  |

### Accessibility Pipeline

[](#accessibility-pipeline)

| Step                          | Feature                                         | Status      | Tier              |
| ----------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | ----------- | ----------------- |
| 1\. **Audit**                 | Read existing PDF tags, detect untagged PDFs    | Shipped     | Free              |
| 2\. **Auto-tag → Tagged PDF** | Generate structure tags for untagged PDFs       | Shipped     | Free (Apache 2.0) |
| 3\. **Export PDF/UA**         | Convert to PDF/UA-1 or PDF/UA-2 compliant files | 💼 Available | Enterprise        |
| 4\. **Visual editing**        | Accessibility studio — review and fix tags      | 💼 Available | Enterprise        |

> **💼 Enterprise features** are available on request. [Contact us](https://opendataloader.org/contact) to get started.

### Auto-Tagging

[](#auto-tagging)

Generate Tagged PDFs from untagged PDFs — output is a screen-reader-ready PDF with structure tags (headings, paragraphs, lists, tables, reading order).

import opendataloader_pdf

# Untagged PDF in → Tagged PDF out
opendataloader_pdf.convert(
    input_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    output_dir="output/",
    format="tagged-pdf"
)

# CLI
opendataloader-pdf --format tagged-pdf file1.pdf file2.pdf folder/

Combine with other formats: `format="json,tagged-pdf"`.

### End-to-End Compliance Workflow

[](#end-to-end-compliance-workflow)

```
Existing PDFs (untagged)
    │
    ▼
┌─────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐    ┌─────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐
│  1. Audit       │───>│  2. Auto-Tag     │───>│  3. Export      │───>│  4. Studio       │
│  (check tags)   │    │  (→ Tagged PDF)  │    │  (PDF/UA)       │    │  (visual editor) │
└─────────────────┘    └──────────────────┘    └─────────────────┘    └──────────────────┘
        │                       │                       │                      │
        ▼                       ▼                       ▼                      ▼
  use_struct_tree      format="tagged-pdf"        PDF/UA export       Accessibility Studio
  (Available now)      (Available, Apache 2.0)    (Enterprise)        (Enterprise)
```

[PDF Accessibility Guide](https://opendataloader.org/docs/accessibility-compliance)

## Frequently Asked Questions

[](#frequently-asked-questions)

### What is the best PDF parser for RAG?

[](#what-is-the-best-pdf-parser-for-rag)

For RAG pipelines, you need a parser that preserves document structure, maintains correct reading order, and provides element coordinates for citations. OpenDataLoader is designed specifically for this — it outputs structured JSON with bounding boxes, handles multi-column layouts with XY-Cut++, and runs locally without GPU. In hybrid mode, it ranks #1 overall (0.907) in benchmarks.

### What is the best open-source PDF parser?

[](#what-is-the-best-open-source-pdf-parser)

OpenDataLoader PDF is the only open-source parser that combines: rule-based deterministic extraction (no GPU), bounding boxes for every element, XY-Cut++ reading order, built-in AI safety filters, native Tagged PDF support, and hybrid AI mode for complex documents. It ranks #1 in overall accuracy (0.907) while running locally on CPU.

### How do I extract tables from PDF for LLM?

[](#how-do-i-extract-tables-from-pdf-for-llm)

OpenDataLoader detects tables using border analysis and text clustering, preserving row/column structure. For complex tables, enable hybrid mode for +90% accuracy improvement (0.489 to 0.928 TEDS score):

# Batch all files in one call — each convert() spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader_pdf.convert(
    input_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    output_dir="output/",
    format="json",
    hybrid="docling-fast"           # For complex tables
)

### How does it compare to docling, marker, or pymupdf4llm?

[](#how-does-it-compare-to-docling-marker-or-pymupdf4llm)

OpenDataLoader \[hybrid\] ranks #1 overall (0.907) across reading order, table, and heading accuracy. Key differences: docling (0.882) is strong but lacks bounding boxes and AI safety filters. marker (0.861) requires GPU and is 1000x slower (53.932s/page). pymupdf4llm (0.732) is fast but has poor table (0.401) and heading (0.412) accuracy. OpenDataLoader is the only parser that combines deterministic local extraction, bounding boxes for every element, and built-in prompt injection protection. See [full benchmark](https://github.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-bench).

### Can I use this without sending data to the cloud?

[](#can-i-use-this-without-sending-data-to-the-cloud)

Yes. OpenDataLoader runs 100% locally. No API calls, no data transmission — your documents never leave your environment. The hybrid mode backend also runs locally on your machine. Ideal for legal, healthcare, and financial documents.

### Does it support OCR for scanned PDFs?

[](#does-it-support-ocr-for-scanned-pdfs)

Yes, via hybrid mode. Install with `pip install "opendataloader-pdf[hybrid]"`, start the backend with `--force-ocr`, then process as usual. Supports multiple languages including Korean, Japanese, Chinese, Arabic, and more via `--ocr-lang`.

### Does it work with Korean, Japanese, or Chinese documents?

[](#does-it-work-with-korean-japanese-or-chinese-documents)

Yes. For digital PDFs, text extraction works out of the box. For scanned PDFs, use hybrid mode with `--force-ocr --ocr-lang "ko,en"` (or `ja`, `ch_sim`, `ch_tra`). Coming soon: [Hancom Data Loader](https://sdk.hancom.com/en/services/1?utm_source=github&utm_medium=readme&utm_campaign=opendataloader-pdf) integration — enterprise-grade AI document analysis with built-in production-grade OCR and customer-customized models optimized for your specific document types and workflows.

### How fast is it?

[](#how-fast-is-it)

Local mode processes 60+ pages per second on CPU (0.02s/page). Hybrid mode processes 2+ pages per second (0.46s/page) with significantly higher accuracy for complex documents. No GPU required. Benchmarked on Apple M4. [Full benchmark details](https://github.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-bench). With multi-process batch processing, throughput exceeds 100 pages per second on 8+ core machines.

### Does it handle multi-column layouts?

[](#does-it-handle-multi-column-layouts)

Yes. OpenDataLoader uses XY-Cut++ reading order analysis to correctly sequence text across multi-column pages, sidebars, and mixed layouts. This works in both local and hybrid modes without any configuration.

### What is hybrid mode?

[](#what-is-hybrid-mode)

Hybrid mode combines fast local Java processing with an AI backend. Simple pages are processed locally (0.02s/page); complex pages (tables, scanned content, formulas, charts) are automatically routed to the AI backend for higher accuracy. The backend runs locally on your machine — no cloud required. See [Which Mode Should I Use?](#which-mode-should-i-use) and [Hybrid Mode Guide](https://opendataloader.org/docs/hybrid-mode).

### Does it work with LangChain?

[](#does-it-work-with-langchain)

Yes. Install `langchain-opendataloader-pdf` for an official LangChain document loader integration. See [LangChain docs](https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/integrations/document_loaders/opendataloader_pdf).

### How do I chunk PDFs for RAG?

[](#how-do-i-chunk-pdfs-for-rag)

OpenDataLoader outputs structured Markdown with headings, tables, and lists preserved — ideal input for semantic chunking. Each element in JSON output includes `type`, `heading level`, and `page number`, so you can split by section or page boundary. For most RAG pipelines: parse with `format="markdown"` for text chunks, or `format="json"` when you need element-level control. Pair with LangChain's `RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter` or your own heading-based splitter for best results.

### How do I cite PDF sources in RAG answers?

[](#how-do-i-cite-pdf-sources-in-rag-answers)

Every element in JSON output includes a `bounding box` (`[left, bottom, right, top]` in PDF points) and `page number`. When your RAG pipeline returns an answer, map the source chunk back to its bounding box to highlight the exact location in the original PDF. This enables "click to source" UX — users see which paragraph, table, or figure the answer came from. No other open-source parser provides bounding boxes for every element by default.

### How do I convert PDF to Markdown for LLM?

[](#how-do-i-convert-pdf-to-markdown-for-llm)

import opendataloader_pdf

# Batch all files in one call — each convert() spawns a JVM process, so repeated calls are slow
opendataloader_pdf.convert(
    input_path=["file1.pdf", "file2.pdf", "folder/"],
    output_dir="output/",
    format="markdown"
)

OpenDataLoader preserves heading hierarchy, table structure, and reading order in the Markdown output. For complex documents with borderless tables or scanned pages, use hybrid mode (`hybrid="docling-fast"`) for higher accuracy. The output is clean enough to feed directly into LLM context windows or RAG chunking pipelines.

### Is there an automated PDF accessibility remediation tool?

[](#is-there-an-automated-pdf-accessibility-remediation-tool)

Yes. OpenDataLoader is the first open-source tool that automates PDF accessibility end-to-end. Built in collaboration with [PDF Association](https://pdfa.org) and [Dual Lab](https://duallab.com) (veraPDF developers), auto-tagging follows the Well-Tagged PDF specification and is validated programmatically using veraPDF. The layout analysis engine detects document structure (headings, tables, lists, reading order) and generates accessibility tags automatically. Auto-tagging converts untagged PDFs into Tagged PDFs under Apache 2.0 — no proprietary SDK dependency. Use `format="tagged-pdf"` (Python/Node.js) or `--format tagged-pdf` (CLI). For organizations needing full PDF/UA compliance, enterprise add-ons provide PDF/UA export and a visual tag editor. This replaces manual remediation workflows that typically cost $50–200+ per document.

### Is this really the first open-source PDF auto-tagging tool?

[](#is-this-really-the-first-open-source-pdf-auto-tagging-tool)

Yes. Existing tools either depend on proprietary SDKs for writing structure tags, only output non-PDF formats (e.g., Docling outputs Markdown/JSON but cannot produce Tagged PDFs), or require manual intervention. OpenDataLoader is the first to do layout analysis → tag generation → Tagged PDF output entirely under an open-source license (Apache 2.0), with no proprietary dependency. Auto-tagging follows the PDF Association's Well-Tagged PDF specification and is validated using veraPDF, the industry-reference open-source PDF/A and PDF/UA validator.

### How do I convert existing PDFs to PDF/UA?

[](#how-do-i-convert-existing-pdfs-to-pdfua)

OpenDataLoader provides an end-to-end pipeline: audit existing PDFs for tags (`use_struct_tree=True`), auto-tag untagged PDFs into Tagged PDFs (`format="tagged-pdf"`, free under Apache 2.0), and export as PDF/UA-1 or PDF/UA-2 (enterprise add-on). Auto-tagging follows the PDF Association's Well-Tagged PDF specification and is validated using veraPDF. Auto-tagging generates the Tagged PDF; PDF/UA export is the final step. [Contact us](https://opendataloader.org/contact) for enterprise integration.

### How do I make my PDFs accessible for EAA compliance?

[](#how-do-i-make-my-pdfs-accessible-for-eaa-compliance)

The European Accessibility Act requires accessible digital products by June 28, 2025. OpenDataLoader supports the full remediation workflow: audit → auto-tag → Tagged PDF → PDF/UA export. Auto-tagging follows the PDF Association's Well-Tagged PDF specification and is validated using veraPDF, ensuring standards-compliant output. Auto-tagging to Tagged PDF is open-source under Apache 2.0. PDF/UA export and accessibility studio are enterprise add-ons. See our [Accessibility Guide](https://opendataloader.org/docs/accessibility-compliance).

### Is OpenDataLoader PDF free?

[](#is-opendataloader-pdf-free)

The core library is **open-source under Apache 2.0** — free for commercial use. This includes all extraction features (text, tables, images, OCR, formulas, charts via hybrid mode), AI safety filters, Tagged PDF support, and auto-tagging to Tagged PDF. We are committed to keeping the core accessibility pipeline (layout analysis → auto-tagging → Tagged PDF) free and open-source. Enterprise add-ons (PDF/UA export, accessibility studio) are available for organizations needing end-to-end regulatory compliance.

### Why did the license change from MPL 2.0 to Apache 2.0?

[](#why-did-the-license-change-from-mpl-20-to-apache-20)

MPL 2.0 requires file-level copyleft, which often triggers legal review before enterprise adoption. Apache 2.0 is fully permissive — no copyleft obligations, easier to integrate into commercial projects. If you are using a pre-2.0 version, it remains under MPL 2.0 and you can continue using it. Upgrading to 2.0+ means your project follows Apache 2.0 terms, which are strictly more permissive — no additional obligations, no action needed on your side.

### 37. tool_result

1→# Onboarding a new bank (seed → golden)
2→
3→A repeatable, security-respecting recipe for adding a new issuer's savings/transaction statements,
4→built on the concept-core parser ([`parser-architecture.md`](./parser-architecture.md)). Most of the
5→time it's **adding a profile (data), not writing a parser**.
6→
7→Two principles do the heavy lifting:
8→- **Reconciliation is the oracle** — a parse is trusted only if the running balance walks (or the
9→  totals match the printed opening/closing). So a wrong parse is *rejected*, never stored.
10→- **Real statements never leave the machine, ever committed.** We validate on local data and ship
11→  *synthetic* fixtures (real layout, fabricated values) for cloud CI.
12→
13→## The loop
14→
15→### 1. Seed (2–3 real statements) — build/tune the profile
16→Put a few real statements of the new bank in a **local, gitignored** folder (e.g.
17→`testdata/<bank>-statements/`). Add a `Profile` in `muneem/parsers/profiles.py` — *data only*:
18→concept↔label aliases, accepted date patterns, account-number regex, quirks. For a transaction-table
19→bank, that profile usually runs straight through the shared engine path (`savings.py`); only add a
20→new parser module if `can_handle` / DB-table wiring is needed (mostly boilerplate, see `au.py`).
21→
22→Iterate with the harness until the seeds reconcile:
23→
24→```bash
25→muneem eval testdata/<bank>-statements/        # uses password_rules.yaml / keychain to unlock
26→```
27→
28→It prints, per file, the outcome (`reconciled` / `unreconciled` / `locked` / `no-parser` / `error`),
29→the row count, and the parser — then an overall **reconcile rate**. No statement values are printed.
30→Tune the profile (and, if needed, the clusterer) until your seeds read `reconciled`. The seeds
31→*prove* the parser; they stay local.
32→
33→> If a vintage won't reconcile, that's the oracle doing its job — it stores nothing rather than
34→> mislabeled rows. Diagnose with the (masked) clustering tools; the `unreconciled` files are safe.
35→
36→### 2. Golden (2–5 real statements) — the high-confidence eval set
37→The reconciling parses are **self-labeling gold**: reconciliation *is* the label. Keep a wider set of
38→real statements locally; they run in the **Layer-2 self-hosted CI** (real data stays on your machine,
39→GitHub only orchestrates — see ROADMAP § "Planned — Regression CI"). `muneem eval` on the golden
40→folder is the quick local gate.
41→
42→### 3. Synthetic fixture — gate the parser in cloud CI
43→Add a born-digital fixture in the new bank's **layout** with **fabricated** data, using the stdlib
44→generator `tests/synthpdf.py`, and assert a full-pipeline `reconciled` (see
45→`tests/test_regression_synth.py`). Commit it to `testdata/synthetic/` — it's safe (no real data) and
46→runs on every push (Layer-1), so the parser is regression-gated without exposing statements.
47→
48→### 4. Land
49→A PR adds: the `Profile` (+ a thin parser module if required) + the synthetic fixture. The golden
50→real statements are registered locally for Layer-2 — **never committed** (`.gitignore` +
51→`.githooks/precommit_check.py` enforce this).
52→
53→## Checklist
54→- [ ] Real seeds in a gitignored folder; password in `password_rules.yaml` (config dir) or keychain.
55→- [ ] `Profile` added (aliases / date patterns / account regex / quirks).
56→- [ ] `muneem eval <seed-folder>` → seeds `reconciled`.
57→- [ ] `muneem eval <golden-folder>` → golden set `reconciled` (your local high-confidence set).
58→- [ ] Synthetic fixture in `testdata/synthetic/` + a `test_regression_synth`-style assertion (Layer-1).
59→- [ ] PR = profile (+ parser module if needed) + synthetic fixture. No real statements committed.
60→

### 38. tool_result

1→from __future__ import annotations
2→
3→from .base import StatementParser, safe_amount
4→
5→_REGISTRY: list[type[StatementParser]] = []
6→
7→
8→def register_parser(parser_cls: type[StatementParser]) -> type[StatementParser]:
9→    """Decorator to register a parser."""
10→    _REGISTRY.append(parser_cls)
11→    return parser_cls
12→
13→
14→def get_parser(
15→    text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None
16→) -> StatementParser | None:
17→    """Return an instantiated parser that can handle this document, or None."""
18→    for parser_cls in _REGISTRY:
19→        parser = parser_cls()
20→        if parser.can_handle(text, sender, subject):
21→            return parser
22→    return None
23→
24→
25→def _autoload_parsers() -> None:
26→    """Import every parser submodule so its ``@register_parser`` runs.
27→
28→    Registration is an import *side-effect*, so the registry is only populated for
29→    modules that have actually been imported. Without this, the production path
30→    (``from muneem.parsers import get_parser``, as cli.py uses) would leave
31→    ``_REGISTRY`` EMPTY — only the tests imported the submodules. Auto-discovery
32→    fixes that and means new parser modules register themselves with no edit here.
33→    """
34→    import importlib
35→    import pkgutil
36→
37→    for info in pkgutil.iter_modules(__path__):
38→        if info.name != "base" and not info.name.startswith("_"):
39→            importlib.import_module(f"{__name__}.{info.name}")
40→
41→
42→_autoload_parsers()
43→
44→__all__ = ["StatementParser", "register_parser", "get_parser", "safe_amount"]
45→

### 47. assistant

**Yes — there are strong positive signals for a structured evaluation.** I did a full deep read of the repo (DESIGN.md, CLAUDE.md, all docs/, pyproject.toml, the entire `src/muneem/parsers/` concept-core + HDFC/Axis/AU paths, `extract.py`, `ocr.py`, `unlock.py`/`statement_unlock.py`, CLI ingest, clustering/engine/validation, tests, license enforcement, synthetic fixtures, etc.) plus targeted investigation of the opendataloader-pdf project (GitHub, README, site docs, benchmarks, Python surface, LangChain loader, announcements).

I have written the findings up as a self-contained project proposal at [docs/proposals/opendataloader-pdf-integration.md](/home/avidullu/projects/Verifiers/muneem/muneem/docs/proposals/opendataloader-pdf-integration.md). It follows the style and rigor of the existing design docs (evidence-grounded, explicit constraints, tables for trade-offs, reversible plan, no silent decisions).

### Executive summary of the deep read + fit

**muneem's PDF reality (what any candidate must satisfy):**
- Stage 2 (unlock) + Stage 3 (parse) are dominated by **password-protected Indian bank/CC/broker PDFs** (name+DOB/PAN-derived passwords via the hybrid resolver in `statement_unlock.py` + `unlock.py` (pypdf) → in-memory handoff).
- Extraction is split: `pdfminer.six` (MIT) for text layer / `can_handle` / SUMMARY / account numbers (`extract.py`); **pdfplumber (MIT)** for the real work — `extract_tables()` for the generic savings engine path (Axis/AU via `savings.py` + `engine.py`) **and** raw `extract_words()` coordinate clustering for HDFC (`hdfc.py` + `clustering.py`) because its semi-ruled tables defeat `extract_tables()`.
- The "secret sauce" is the **concept-core + reconciliation oracle** (profiles-as-data, L0/L1/L2 mapping search, per-row balance walk + total reconcile + bookend vs printed SUMMARY in `validation.py`). Extraction *proposes*; arithmetic *disposes*. Unreconciled parses store nothing (oracle-gated safety for real sensitive data).
- Strict license regime (only MIT/BSD/Apache-2.0/ISC/HPND; AGPL banned and enforced by `tests/test_dependency_licenses.py` + `docs/DEPENDENCIES.md`; PyMuPDF explicitly called out as forbidden). Python-only v1. Local-by-default for sensitive categories (promo corpus is the safe playground). Future L3 local-vision path already researched (DeepSeek-OCR-3B MIT + Qwen Apache, reconcile-gated).

**OpenDataLoader PDF (key facts from project sources):**
- Current license: **Apache 2.0** (deliberate move from earlier MPL-2.0 for commercial friendliness). This is on the allowlist.
- Local-first by design ("100% locally... no data transmission... ideal for ... financial documents"). Hybrid mode runs a *local* backend (you start `opendataloader-pdf-hybrid`); no cloud required.
- Explicit **encrypted/password-protected PDF support** (CLI `-p` / `--password`, documented in LangChain loader, Java config surface).
- Strong on the exact pain points: table extraction (simple borders in base; complex/borderless + merged cells in hybrid; claims 0.928 TEDS in their benchmarks), XY-Cut++ reading order, **bounding boxes on every element** (`[left, bottom, right, top]` + page), semantic structure (headings, tables as first-class, etc.).
- Outputs: Markdown + rich JSON (perfect for RAG/citations) + HTML + auto-tagged PDF (free under Apache).
- Hybrid adds local OCR (80+ langs, `--force-ocr`), formula, chart descriptions. Base mode is fast deterministic Java; hybrid routes hard pages to local AI add-ons for the big accuracy jump (their overall hybrid #1 at 0.907).
- Python client exists (`opendataloader_pdf.convert`, batch-oriented); each top-level call spawns a JVM (docs warn this is slow for repeated single calls; batch or the long-running hybrid server is the intended model). Requires Java 11+.
- Public benchmarks + data; backed by Hancom (real PDF company) + PDF Association / veraPDF collaboration for the accessibility side.

**Fit signals (positive and material):**
- License + security posture line up extremely well.
- Password handling is a solved problem in the candidate (muneem's resolver can supply the string).
- Table + geometry + reading-order strengths directly attack the custom code that was written because pdfplumber's `extract_tables` was unreliable on real HDFC layouts and vintages drift on Axis.
- The propose-then-reconcile philosophy is compatible (a stronger proposer + same verifier = lower risk).
- Promo corpus + synthetic fixtures + the existing `muneem eval` + local-golden harness give a perfect, zero-risk way to measure on both non-sensitive and real-layout (local-only) data.
- Scanned/image path (hybrid OCR) aligns with planned L3 work.

**Real mismatches / risks (called out plainly in the proposal):**
- **Impedance mismatch** is the biggest: muneem's engine/clustering/HDFC path are tightly coupled to pdfplumber cell-matrices and word dicts. ODL's primary surface is higher-level semantic JSON + tables + bboxes, not a drop-in raw word stream. Integration is likely "ODL as another candidate proposer" (or dual paths) rather than a leaf swap.
- JVM-per-call cost and the hybrid daemon (if you want the headline table/OCR numbers) are operational departures from the current tiny pure-Python + Tesseract setup.
- Numeric fidelity on adversarial statement layouts (exact paise amounts, running-balance walks, SUMMARY bookends, continuation-line narration, value dates in the narration band) is not proven by general benchmarks. The oracle will protect storage, but quarantine rate on real vintages matters.
- New hard dep (JDK 11+). Transitive license cleanliness of hybrid AI bits needs audit even if the core is Apache.
- Current stack has real investment and measured success on the user's statements (HDFC 6/6, AU 4/4, Axis 6/7 with oracle safety). Replacement cost is non-trivial.

**Overall**: Not an obvious "just pip install and delete pdfplumber" win, but **clearly worth a scoped, reversible, evidence-driven evaluation** before any architectural commitment. The signals are strong enough on the things muneem treats as non-negotiable (local, license, passwords, financial sensitivity, table quality on messy real PDFs) that walking away without data would be leaving value on the table.

### Recommended next step
Read the full proposal (it includes a concrete 3-phase eval plan using only safe data + the existing harness, success criteria, decision gate, and explicit open questions). If you want to proceed, the first two phases (license hygiene + side-by-side on promo + synth fixtures) are low-effort and can be done without touching production paths or real statements.

I can kick off the Phase 0/1 spike (adapter to normalize ODL JSON into something the engine can consume, run the existing tests + `muneem eval` equivalents, capture metrics) once you give direction. Or we can refine the proposal first.

The file is at `docs/proposals/opendataloader-pdf-integration.md` and is ready to be iterated or referenced in discussion. All claims are attributed; nothing was assumed. Let me know how you'd like to move.

1→# Proposal: Evaluate OpenDataLoader PDF as a PDF extraction provider for muneem
2→
3→> Status: **Draft for discussion.** Positive signals identified during a full-repo deep read (2026-06). No integration work has started. This document surfaces the evidence, the fit analysis against muneem's hard constraints, risks, and a concrete low-risk evaluation plan. Do not treat any part as a decision.
4→
5→**Context**: muneem's Stage 3 (Document parsing) is the acknowledged "hard 80%". The current implementation relies on a small, permissive, pure-Python stack (`pdfminer.six`, `pdfplumber`, `pypdf`) plus custom coordinate clustering and a concept-mapping + reconciliation engine. The question is whether https://github.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-pdf (Apache-2.0, local-first, strong table/reading-order claims, explicit encrypted-PDF support) is worth a serious look as a replacement or complementary provider.
6→
7→---
8→
9→## 1. Current PDF extraction reality in muneem (grounded in code)
10→
11→**Hard requirements the stack must satisfy today** (derived from DESIGN.md §4–5, parser-architecture.md, ingesting-statements.md, actual implementation):
12→
13→- **Password-protected PDFs** (ubiquitous for Indian bank/CC/broker statements). Hybrid sourcing (keychain + `password_rules.yaml` + prompt) resolves the password; it is forwarded *in-memory only* (never logged, never written). See `statement_unlock.py`, `unlock.py` (pypdf for `is_encrypted`/`decrypt`), `hdfc.py:273`, `savings.py:183`.
14→- **Text layer** for `can_handle` heuristics, account-number extraction (via profiles), and SUMMARY blocks used by the verifier (`extract.py` uses pdfminer.six; parsers call `page.extract_text()`).
15→- **Table recovery or word-level geometry** at high fidelity:
16→  - `pdfplumber.extract_tables()` for the generic savings path (Axis, AU Bank) → fed to `engine.map_table` (L0 header lexicon + profile + L2 content search + oracle).
17→  - `page.extract_words(use_text_flow=False, ...)` for HDFC's bespoke coordinate clustering (`hdfc.py`) because its transaction table is "semi-ruled" and `extract_tables()` is unreliable. See `clustering.py` (`group_lines`, `infer_columns` via coverage histogram, `cluster_to_matrix` with `keep_line` predicate to stay in the transaction band).
18→  - Balance-delta reconstruction fallback when columns are messy (merged amount, interleaved value-date, sparse deposit).
19→- **Reconciliation as the oracle** (never trust extraction alone). Per-row running-balance walk + total reconcile + bookend against printed SUMMARY. A parse that does not reconcile stores **nothing** (`UNRECONCILED` / quarantine). This is what makes the whole system safe on real (sensitive) data without eyeballing values. See `validation.py`, `savings.py:reconcile_oracle`, `hdfc.py:validate_against_summary`.
20→- **License strictness** (DESIGN §5 rule 8, `docs/DEPENDENCIES.md`, `tests/test_dependency_licenses.py`): only MIT / BSD / Apache-2.0 / ISC / HPND (sqlcipher3 is a documented Zlib exception). PyMuPDF/fitz is explicitly banned (AGPL). Adding anything requires updating both the table *and* the allowlist test, or CI fails.
21→- **Local by default for sensitive categories** (bank, CC, broker, MF, insurance). Promo corpus is the non-sensitive playground. No cloud LLM without explicit per-category approval in-session.
22→- **Python v1** (resolved). Java ingestion + Python parse was considered but not chosen.
23→- **Future needs**: scanned/image statements (L3 local-vision fallback research already points at DeepSeek-OCR-3B (MIT) + Qwen2.5-VL-7B (Apache), reconcile-gated); itemized receipts; holdings statements.
24→
25→Current leaf libraries (all allowed):
26→- `pdfminer.six` (MIT) — text layer.
27→- `pdfplumber` (MIT) — tables + positioned words.
28→- `pypdf` (BSD-3) — encryption detection + decryption.
29→- `pypdfium2` (Apache/BSD) + `pytesseract` — OCR rendering for promo banners (and future scanned statements).
30→
31→The concept-core (engine + profiles-as-data + verifier) is deliberately **extraction-agnostic above the cell-matrix boundary**. Adding a new extraction source is theoretically "just another way to propose candidate matrices/rows that the oracle then judges."
32→
33→---
34→
35→## 2. What OpenDataLoader PDF is (evidence from primary sources)
36→
37→- **License**: Apache License 2.0 (current; pre-2.0 releases were MPL-2.0). The project explicitly moved to Apache "to lower the barrier... for commercial use." Full LICENSE in repo is the standard Apache 2.0 text. This is on muneem's allowlist.
38→- **Core**: Primarily Java (≈86% of the tree), with official thin Python client (`pip install opendataloader-pdf`), Node, and Java SDKs. Python usage: `import opendataloader_pdf; opendataloader_pdf.convert(...)`.
39→- **Runtime**: Requires Java 11+ (Adoptium recommended). Each top-level `convert()` call spawns a JVM process (docs repeatedly warn this makes repeated calls slow; batching is the intended pattern). Hybrid mode runs a separate long-lived `opendataloader-pdf-hybrid` backend server (local).
40→- **Password/encrypted support**: Explicit. CLI: `opendataloader-pdf encrypted.pdf -p mypassword ...`. LangChain loader documents `password=` param. Java Config exposes password for opening encrypted files. (This is table stakes for muneem.)
41→- **Outputs**: Markdown (for RAG/chunking), JSON (semantic elements with `type`, `page number`, `bounding box` as `[left, bottom, right, top]` in PDF points, plus per-element attributes), HTML, plain text, Annotated PDF (debug), and `tagged-pdf` (auto-tagging untagged PDFs into screen-reader structure under the free Apache license).
42→- **Capabilities (local deterministic + hybrid)**:
43→  - Text with correct reading order (XY-Cut++ for multi-column/sidebars).
44→  - Bounding boxes on *every* element.
45→  - Table extraction: "simple borders" in base mode; "complex/borderless", merged cells via hybrid.
46→  - Heading hierarchy, lists, images (with coords).
47→  - OCR for scanned/image PDFs (80+ languages) in hybrid (`--force-ocr`, `--ocr-lang`).
48→  - Formula (LaTeX) and chart/image description enrichments in hybrid.
49→  - AI safety filters (hidden text, off-page, prompt-injection patterns); optional sanitization.
50→  - Tagged-PDF structure extraction when present (`use_struct_tree`).
51→- **Hybrid mode**: Local AI backend (no cloud by default). Simple pages stay in fast deterministic Java path (~0.02s/page claimed); complex pages route to the AI add-ons for big accuracy gains. Marketed as "ideal for legal, healthcare, and financial documents."
52→- **Benchmarks** (project's own reproducible suite on 200 real-world PDFs): hybrid ranks #1 overall (0.907), with standout table score (0.928). Base local mode is much faster but weaker on tables (0.489 in their numbers). Public benchmark repo + data.
53→- **Positioning**: Built for RAG (structured Markdown + JSON + citations via bboxes), accessibility automation (first open-source end-to-end auto-tagging to Tagged PDF, validated with veraPDF + PDF Association collaboration), and "AI-ready data."
54→
55→No evidence in the fetched materials of a low-level "give me every positioned word token like pdfplumber.extract_words()" API; the surface is curated semantic elements + tables + bboxes.
56→
57→---
58→
59→## 3. Positive signals (why this is worth a serious look)
60→
61→1. **License alignment is near-perfect**. Apache 2.0 is explicitly permitted. The project made a deliberate license change from MPL-2.0 to reduce enterprise friction. Adding it would require only the normal DEPENDENCIES.md + allowlist-test update (no banned substrings).
62→2. **Security posture matches muneem's non-negotiables**. 100% local by default. "No API calls, no data transmission... Ideal for ... financial documents." Hybrid backend is user-run on the local machine. This is the language muneem uses in DESIGN §5 and CLAUDE.md.
63→3. **Password-protected workflow is first-class**. The exact capability muneem had to build around (Indian bank PDFs) is supported with a documented password parameter. muneem's existing hybrid resolver (`resolve_password`) can supply the string; decryption would move inside ODL for files routed through it.
64→4. **Table extraction is the headline strength** — and muneem's biggest custom-code tax. HDFC required an entire bespoke clustering module because `extract_tables()` was unreliable on a real, high-volume semi-ruled layout. Axis vintages vary enough that the old fixed-column approach produced confidently wrong rows (the oracle correctly quarantined them). ODL claims 0.928 table accuracy in hybrid and explicitly calls out merged cells + complex/borderless tables. If it can propose better candidate matrices for the existing engine (or better semantic table objects), the reconcile oracle still provides the safety net.
65→5. **Geometry + reading order are exposed** (bboxes on every element + XY-Cut++). This is directly relevant to `clustering.py`'s coverage-histogram column inference, continuation-line attachment, and any future provenance/citation features. Even if not a 1:1 replacement for raw word dicts, it may be higher-signal input for the L0/L1/L2 ladder.
66→6. **Scanned / image path exists** (hybrid + OCR). Aligns with the planned L3 local-vision fallback (research already selected DeepSeek-OCR-3B (MIT) + Qwen (Apache), reconcile-gated). Promo corpus (heavy banner imagery, some codes only in images) is a perfect non-sensitive testbed.
67→7. **Python surface + LangChain precedent**. Official client exists. The LangChain loader already documents the `password` parameter — evidence the integration point has been exercised by others.
68→8. **"Extraction proposes, reconciliation disposes" philosophy is compatible**. ODL (even in hybrid) is still an *extractor*. muneem never trusts any extractor on money without the arithmetic oracle. Using a stronger proposer while keeping the verifier unchanged is low-risk in principle.
69→9. **Non-sensitive corpus is immediately usable**. `testdata/promo-emails/` (text-layer codes + image-only codes + negatives) can validate OCR, table-ish structures in marketing PDFs, reading order, and dedup potential with zero policy violation. Synthetic statement fixtures (`tests/synthpdf.py`) and the `muneem eval` harness give a grounded path for real-layout testing without committing data.
70→10. **Maturity signals**. Backed by Hancom (real PDF company), public benchmarks with code+data, active releases (v2.x), large claimed community, explicit collaboration with veraPDF/PDF Association people. Not a tiny weekend project.
71→
72→---
73→
74→## 4. Risks, mismatches, and open questions (material — do not gloss over)
75→
76→**Architecture impedance (highest risk)**:
77→- muneem's core (`engine.py`, `clustering.py`, `savings.py`, `hdfc.py`, `validation.py`) is written against pdfplumber's concrete shapes: list-of-rows-of-cells from `extract_tables()`, and lists of `{"text", "x0", "x1", "top", "bottom"}` word dicts.
78→- HDFC's production path is *deliberately* not using `extract_tables()`; the clusterer + line grouping + x-band logic is the reference implementation for semi-ruled cases.
79→- ODL's primary outputs are higher-level semantic JSON (elements with type/bbox/content, tables as structured objects) + Markdown. It is unclear (from public docs) whether there is a supported path to the raw positioned token stream at the fidelity the current clusterer and balance-delta reconstructor expect.
80→- Integration would likely be one of: (a) treat ODL as a *competing table proposer* inside the existing engine (feed its table JSON or bbox-derived cells as another candidate to `map_table` + oracle); (b) keep pdfplumber for the HDFC clustering path and use ODL only for the generic savings path + future issuers; or (c) a larger refactor to make the "proposer" pluggable at a higher semantic level. Any of these has cost and regression surface.
81→
82→**Operational / deployment model**:
83→- JVM spawn per `convert()` call (explicitly called out as slow for repeated use). muneem's `ingest` path is per-document (after unlock). Batch APIs help, but interactive/backfill UX and the CLI matter.
84→- Hybrid (the mode that delivers the headline table/OCR numbers) requires running a separate local server process + (for best results) model assets. Acceptable under "local", but adds a daemon and resource profile that pure stdlib + Tesseract does not have.
85→- New hard systems dependency: JDK 11+. Currently muneem needs only Python + Tesseract binary on PATH.
86→
87→**Numeric fidelity on adversarial financial layouts**:
88→- Bank statements are full of right-aligned currency amounts that must parse to the exact paise, running balances that must walk without drift, and SUMMARY blocks used for bookending. General-document benchmarks (even strong ones) do not prove this. The reconcile oracle will catch many errors (that's its job), but a weaker base extractor could increase the quarantine rate on real vintages (e.g. the still-open Axis May 2025 dropped-line case).
89→- Need concrete evidence on *muneem's* layouts and vintages before claiming a win.
90→
91→**Password + decryption contract**:
92→- ODL accepts a password and (presumably) decrypts internally before extraction. muneem's current flow uses pypdf to *test* candidates and then hands the winner to pdfplumber/pdfminer. If ODL becomes the primary extractor for a document, we may be able to stop round-tripping through pypdf for that path — or we keep a dual "unlock then hand plaintext" path. Must verify the Python API surface accepts the password and that decrypted content is what reaches the JSON/tables (no silent fallback to empty on bad pw).
93→
94→**Hybrid AI components & transitive license**:
95→- Even though the project advertises the hybrid add-ons as part of the free Apache offering, the actual models/backends (docling-fast, SmolVLM, OCR engines, etc.) must be audited for GPL/AGPL weights, non-commercial terms, or large binary blobs that would complicate the allowlist test and commercial-safety story. "Local" does not automatically mean "permissive license chain."
96→
97→**Replacement cost vs. incremental value**:
98→- The current stack is tiny, pure-Python, well-understood, and the clustering + engine + verifier have received significant investment and real-statement validation (HDFC 6/6, AU 4/4, Axis 6/7 with oracle safety).
99→- Swapping the leaf for a large Java project has migration, debugging, and "works on my statements" risk. The highest-leverage win may be narrow (better table candidates for the generic path + future issuers + scanned documents) rather than a full rip-and-replace.
100→

### 54. tool_result

1→"""Local-only checks for the AU Bank parser against real encrypted statements.
2→
3→Skipped unless an AU password is available (``password_rules.yaml`` or ``MUNEEM_TEST_AU_PW``); never
4→runs in CI. The parser is **oracle-gated**, so this asserts the safety invariant: it either stored
5→nothing (a vintage it can't yet read) or everything it stored reconciled — never mislabeled rows.
6→Surfaces counts / booleans / parser names only — no statement content.
7→"""
8→
9→import os
10→from pathlib import Path
11→
12→import pdfplumber
13→import pytest
14→
15→from muneem import db as dbmod
16→from muneem.parsers import get_parser
17→from muneem.password_rules import get_password_for_bank
18→
19→AU_PDFS = sorted(Path("testdata/aubank-statements").glob("*.pdf"))
20→
21→
22→@pytest.mark.local_statements
23→@pytest.mark.parametrize("pdf_path", AU_PDFS, ids=lambda p: p.name)
24→def test_local_au_never_stores_unreconciled(pdf_path, tmp_path):
25→    pw = (
26→        get_password_for_bank("aubank")
27→        or get_password_for_bank("au")
28→        or os.environ.get("MUNEEM_TEST_AU_PW")
29→    )
30→    if not pw:
31→        pytest.skip("needs the AU password (password_rules.yaml or MUNEEM_TEST_AU_PW)")
32→
33→    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=pw) as pdf:
34→        text = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(2, len(pdf.pages))))
35→    parser = get_parser(text)
36→    if parser is None or type(parser).__name__ != "AUParser":
37→        pytest.skip(f"{pdf_path.name}: not routed to AUParser")
38→
39→    with dbmod.connect(tmp_path / "t.db") as conn:
40→        dbmod.init_db(conn)
41→        doc_id = dbmod.insert_document(conn, source="file", sha256=pdf_path.name, status="parsed")
42→        n = parser.parse_and_insert(pdf_path, conn, doc_id, password=pw)
43→        status = dbmod.get_document_by_sha(conn, pdf_path.name)["status"]
44→        stored = dbmod.list_au_transactions(conn)
45→
46→    assert (n == 0 and stored == []) or status == "reconciled", (
47→        f"{pdf_path.name}: stored {n} row(s) with status={status} — must not persist unreconciled"
48→    )
49→

1→"""Tests for `muneem eval` — the seed→golden onboarding harness.
2→
3→Runs the CLI against a folder of *synthetic* statements (no secrets, no real data) and checks the
4→reconcile-rate report. Reuses the born-digital fixtures from the regression suite.
5→"""
6→
7→from typer.testing import CliRunner
8→

100→
101→**Open questions to resolve in evaluation (not by assumption)**:
102→- Exact Python API for password-protected files via the direct `opendataloader_pdf.convert` (not just the LangChain wrapper).
103→- Whether low-level word geometry or a pdfplumber-compatible surface is available, or whether we must work exclusively from the semantic JSON + bboxes.
104→- Performance characteristics for the muneem ingest pattern (one PDF at a time, after hybrid unlock) vs. the documented batch preference.
105→- Reconcile rates on real HDFC/Axis/AU vintages (local only) when ODL tables/rows are fed into the existing engine vs. current pdfplumber paths.
106→- License cleanliness of the hybrid extras actually pulled in at runtime.
107→- How ODL's reading order + element classification interacts with muneem's "narration may wrap across continuation lines" and "value date lives in the narration band" realities.
108→
109→---
110→
111→## 5. Proposed evaluation plan (low-risk, high-signal, reversible)
112→
113→**Phase 0 — Paper + license hygiene (no code, 1–2 hours)**
114→- Confirm Apache 2.0 of the exact version we would pin.
115→- Audit the hybrid extra's transitive/runtime deps against the banned-substring rule and DEPENDENCIES.md policy.
116→- Document the exact Python call sites for password + convert.
117→
118→**Phase 1 — Side-by-side on safe data (promo corpus + synthetic fixtures)**
119→- Add an optional extraction backend behind a feature flag or separate module (e.g. `muneem.extract_odl` or an internal adapter that returns a normalized "candidate tables + text + bboxes" shape).
120→- Run the existing promo regression (`test_promo_regression.py`, `test_extract.py`) and OCR tier through ODL (text layer + hybrid OCR) and compare recall/precision/fuzzy OCR match against current pdfminer + pypdfium2 + Tesseract path.
121→- Run synthetic full-pipeline fixtures (`test_regression_synth.py`, `synthpdf.py` — the ones that go through real pdfminer/pdfplumber) with ODL as an alternative proposer; assert the verifier still sees a `reconciled` outcome (or measure delta in quarantine rate).
122→- Measure wall time and JVM spawn behavior on repeated single-file calls vs. batch.
123→
124→**Phase 2 — Local real seeds (the only place that proves bank-statement value)**
125→- Using the existing `muneem eval <folder>` harness + `password_rules.yaml` (or keychain), run a small set of local gitignored real statements (HDFC seeds + golden, plus any Axis/AU that currently stress the clusterer or engine).
126→- Two modes:
127→  - ODL as a *table proposer candidate* inside the current savings/HDFC paths (best apples-to-apples).
128→  - ODL end-to-end (its JSON tables → map to ConceptRows → verifier).
129→- Record: reconcile rate, bookend success, row count delta vs. current parser, specific vintages that improve or regress (especially Axis May/Jun 2025 cases), any numeric drift the oracle catches.
130→- Capture ODL's JSON (or a redacted subset) for offline analysis of bbox quality and reading-order decisions on real statement layouts.
131→
132→**Phase 3 — Decision gate**
133→- Produce a short memo: "ODL on muneem layouts — what it improves, where it is neutral or worse, operational cost, integration surface, remaining unknowns."
134→- Only then discuss adoption strategy (narrow for generic savings path + future issuers? dual extraction with preference for reconciling candidate? full leaf replacement for certain doc types? keep current stack for HDFC clustering?).
135→
136→**Non-goals for the eval**:
137→- Do not change the production `ingest` path or any default until the gate.
138→- Do not commit real statements or passwords.
139→- Do not wire hybrid server into CI or developer setup without explicit approval.
140→- Keep all changes behind opt-in flags / separate test markers so the existing green suite is untouched.
141→
142→**Artifacts**:
143→- A small adapter (throwaway if we walk away) that normalizes ODL JSON into something the engine/verifier can consume.
144→- Updates to `docs/DEPENDENCIES.md` + allowlist only if we decide to adopt.
145→- (Optional) a `docs/parser-notes-opendataloader.md` capturing real-vintage observations.
146→
147→---
148→
149→## 6. Success criteria for "yes, integrate"
150→
151→- License + transitive deps pass the allowlist test with no policy exceptions beyond the existing sqlcipher3 pattern.
152→- On the local real seed/golden set, ODL-as-proposer or ODL-direct produces **equal or higher** reconcile + bookend rate than the current pdfplumber paths (especially on the known-hard vintages).
153→- Numeric fidelity is sufficient that the oracle does not start quarantining statements that currently reconcile (or the increase in quarantine rate is understood and acceptable).
154→- Operational story is acceptable: password wiring is clean, single-file ingest latency is reasonable (or we document a batch-oriented path), Java/JDK requirement is acceptable to the user, hybrid daemon (if used for accuracy) is documented and optional.
155→- The integration cost (adapter surface, dual maintenance, debugging two extractors) is judged lower than the ongoing cost of maintaining custom clustering + tuning profiles for every new issuer/vintage drift.
156→- Clear story for L3 (scanned statements) that reuses the same reconcile gate.
157→
158→If any of the above are not met after a good-faith eval, the rational outcome is "valuable tool, not a fit for muneem's current architecture and constraints" — and we keep the pdfplumber + clustering + engine investment.
159→
160→---
161→
162→## 7. Recommendation
163→
164→**There are sufficiently strong positive signals (license, local posture, encrypted-PDF support, table/reading-order claims, compatibility with the "propose-then-reconcile" philosophy, and a perfect non-sensitive test corpus) that a structured, time-boxed evaluation is justified.**
165→
166→Do **not** start integration work, dependency changes, or refactors based on marketing claims or general benchmarks. The evaluation plan above is deliberately staged so we can stop at any point with zero impact on the mainline, and every real decision is made against muneem's own layouts and the reconcile oracle.
167→
168→Before any code lands, surface this proposal (or the eval memo that follows) for explicit direction, especially on:
169→- Tolerance for a JDK requirement.
170→- Willingness to run a local hybrid backend for the accuracy tier.
171→- Appetite for a dual-extractor world during a transition period.
172→- Whether the primary goal is "better tables for the existing engine" or "simpler parser code overall."
173→
174→This is exactly the kind of leaf-technology decision the project has historically handled with evidence (see the hdfc-parser-tech-decision memory, the L3 VLM research, the encryption-at-rest research, and the concept-core pivot itself).
175→
176→---
177→
178→## Appendix: Quick attribution notes (for the record)
179→
180→- All claims about ODL capabilities, license history, benchmarks, password support, hybrid mode, JVM behavior, and output formats are taken from the project's GitHub README, site docs (opendataloader.org), PyPI, LangChain integration page, and related announcements (crawled June 2026).
181→- muneem internals are from direct source read of `src/muneem/parsers/*`, `extract.py`, `unlock.py`, `statement_unlock.py`, `ocr.py`, `cli.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `docs/*.md`, and the test suite.
182→- No real statement content, passwords, or PII was examined or transmitted during this analysis.
183→
184→**End of proposal.** Ready for discussion and, if approved, a scoped evaluation spike.
